with the command above and `lvrtc compare`. Each figure is the largest of its kind over the rays that are ok in
both engines, in waves of the line: of the path to the last surface, of the path to the image plane, and of the
path relative to the chief ray's:

| Suite | R3, pairs of two engines | optiland against `ref` | `lv` against `ref` |
|---|---|---|---|
| `benchmark`, 216 ray sets | 648 `PASS` | 2.7e-9, 2.7e-9, 2.7e-9: `nikon-z-24-70f4s` wide at 470 nm, 43.3° | 6.0e-6, `sigma-35mm-f14-dg-hsm-a` at 510 nm, 31.9°; 5.3e-6, `sony-fe-20mm-f18-g` at 470 nm, 47.5°; 5.5e-6, `nikon-z-24-70f4s` tele at 470 nm, 16.6° |
| `features`, 162 ray sets | 482 `PASS`, 4 `FLOOR` | 3.0e-9, `fixed-iris-zoom` wide at 39.3°; 3.1e-9, `asphere-a20` at 24.2°; 3.5e-9, `rear-plate-rim` at 17.7°: all at 470 nm | 3.8e-6, `e-line` at 12.1°; 2.07e-5 and 2.28e-5, `stop-inside-element` at 470 nm, 55.3° |
| the contract's three cases and twelve systems made for the two rungs, 51 ray sets, against `ref` | 51 `PASS` | 2.5e-9, a surface set into the curve before it; 1.9e-9, the case of every feature; with a chief ray, in a request of the test's own, 1.7e-9 | no case of LensVisualizer |
| the 24 focus stations LensVisualizer certifies, on the reference line, 72 ray sets | 216 `PASS` | 3.9e-9, `sigma-35mm-f12-dg-ii-art` at its closest focus, 17.8°; 3.0e-9, `fujifilm-gf80-f17`, 18.3°; 3.3e-9, `sigma-50mm-f12-dg-dn-art`, 25.4° | 8.1e-6 and 8.3e-6, `fujifilm-gf80-f17` at its closest focus, 18.3°; 5.9e-6, `sigma-24mm-f2-dg-dn-contemporary`, 44.5° |

`lv` against optiland has the figures of `lv` against `ref` to two digits. The four floors are the Hologon's again,
at its full field at 470 nm and 510 nm, against `ref` and against optiland alike: the ray leaves 54° off the axis,
and LensVisualizer's 2.2e-9 mm on the last surface is 2.07e-5 waves on the image plane. The reason names the
witness:

```
opd.maxAbs 2.28e-5 exceeds its tolerance 2.00e-5 at field 5.53e1, line 1, ray 264; opticalPathToImage.maxAbs
2.07e-5 exceeds its tolerance 2.00e-5 at field 5.53e1, line 1, ray 264; floor of lv: optiland against ref
opd.maxAbs 4.23e-10 within 1.00e-7, optiland against ref opticalPath.maxAbs 6.05e-11 within 1.00e-7, optiland
against ref opticalPathToImage.maxAbs 4.23e-10 within 1.00e-7, lv against ref opd.maxAbs 2.28e-5 within 2.00e-4,
lv against ref opticalPath.maxAbs 1.73e-6 within 2.00e-4, lv against ref opticalPathToImage.maxAbs 2.07e-5 within
2.00e-4
```

The path relative to the chief ray is measured wherever every engine lands that ray: in every set of the
benchmark, in all but six of the feature suite (the full field of `fixed-iris-zoom` at its wide end, where an
aperture stops the chief ray for all three) and in all but one at the stations; there the two paths are judged
alone, and the pair says so. A case file's probe lattice states no chief ray at all. Traced in 60-digit
arithmetic, the rays that set optiland's figures above have optiland 2.7e-9, 3.5e-9 and 3.9e-9 waves from the
truth and `ref` within 1.6e-10; the Hologon's ray has LensVisualizer 2.07e-5 and 2.28e-5 from it, optiland
1.8e-10 and `ref` 6e-11. So in the suites and at the stations optiland agrees with the arbiter twenty-five times
more closely than the floor rule asks of a witness (1e-7 waves), and what it differs by is its own: the hits of its
plain sums on an asphere, which move a path by half of what they are off
([docs/gotchas.md](gotchas.md#optilands-optical-path-is-a-plain-sum-and-as-exact-as-its-hits)).

**Outside the suites it does not always, in R3 as in R2.** On the four lenses on which optiland's hits are no
witness, each on its reference line and the photopic lines, the zoom at both ends (90 ray sets, outside the
tests), every pair of optiland passes R3, and optiland is this far from `ref`, in waves, where the 60-digit trace
puts `ref` within 3.1e-9 of the truth on the worst ray and the rest on optiland:

| Lens | optiland against `ref`: path, to the image, relative | R3 of `lv` against `ref`, alone | beside optiland |
|---|---|---|---|
| `fujifilm-fujinon-xf-8-16mm-f28-r-lm-wr`, wide end | 4.3e-7, 4.5e-7, 4.5e-7 at 62.8°, 610 nm | 14 `PASS`, 4 `FLOOR` | 14 `PASS`, 4 `FAIL` |
| the same, tele end | 1.6e-8, 1.7e-8, 1.8e-8 | 18 `PASS` | 18 `PASS` |
| `fujifilm-fujinon-xf-27mm-f28` | 8.9e-7, 9.7e-7, 9.7e-7 at 27.6°, 510 nm | 16 `PASS`, 2 `FLOOR` | 16 `PASS`, 2 `FAIL` |
| `apple-iphone-7-wide-camera-lens` | 1.7e-8, 1.8e-8, 1.7e-8 at 21.0°, 470 nm | 18 `PASS` | 18 `PASS` |
| `russar-22-70f8` | 1.2e-9, 2.7e-7, 2.8e-7 at 65.1°, 510 nm | 13 `PASS`, 5 `FLOOR` | 13 `PASS`, 2 `FLOOR`, 3 `FAIL` |

Every figure is inside the gate of R3 by a factor of twenty or more. Under policy version 4, which the last
column is of, it cost the floor: 9 of the 11 pairs of `lv` that are `FLOOR` beside `ref` alone were `FAIL` beside
optiland, "optiland does not agree with ref", as 14 of 17 were in R2 on the same sets. LensVisualizer's own
largest figures there are 7.8e-5 waves (the zoom), 1.4e-4 (the 27 mm) and 3.4e-5 (the Russar), all inside the
floor's limit of 2e-4. The owner decided it on these figures: since policy version 5 a witness that does not
corroborate withholds no floor, and is named in the reason; only one that sides with `lv` against `ref` does
([the plan's amendment](IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md#a-witness-does-not-withhold-a-floor)). These lenses are outside
the suites and were not run again for it.

**A far origin** is what would cost optiland's path most, and no ray of LensVisualizer has one. optiland solves a
conic from where the ray is, and half of what that costs the hit is in the path: on a front sphere of radius
30 mm, 2.2e-8 waves from 2.3 m, 6.5e-7 from 16 m and 5.5e-5 from 100 m, beyond the gate; a tenth of that on a
radius of 300 mm. LensVisualizer launches the rays of a finite conjugate from a plane 18 mm to 75 mm in front of
the lens, along the lines from the object point, so a station's paths start there. The probe lattice of a case
file with a finite object does start at the object point
([docs/gotchas.md](gotchas.md#a-conic-is-met-from-where-the-ray-is-and-from-far-away-that-costs-the-square-of-the-distance)).

**What a trace costs**, measured on the benchmark's 216 ray sets (235 812 rays, 18 to 39 surfaces) sent to one
worker one after another: 14 ms a set, of which 0.8 ms are reading the request's arrays and checking them, 2.8 ms
building the line's optic and reading it back, 9 ms optiland's trace and the reading of its rows, and 0.9 ms
writing the answer; 4.8 s for all of them. The first trace of a worker takes 0.08 s more, and 0.9 s on an empty
cache directory, while numba compiles; a run may take ten minutes for a reply. The worker logs each trace on its
standard error (rays, surfaces, how they ended, the four times, its peak memory and its process id): an answer
holds no time. One process serves a run, which a test counts. Its memory is that of optiland's import, 595 MiB
with vtk, matplotlib, scipy and numba, and 700 MiB at its peak over the benchmark, 735 MiB after three passes of
it: a request's optic and arrays are garbage once it is answered, and with that garbage collected the worker
holds the same number of objects after every pass.

**Nothing is written into the optiland checkout or its environment.** Importing optiland imports numba,
matplotlib and vtk, each of which writes somewhere unless told where, so the worker's environment says where,
under the configuration's `cacheDir` (`.cache/optiland`, gitignored):

| Variable | Why |
|---|---|
| `NUMBA_CACHE_DIR` | numba caches the machine code of optiland's `@njit(cache=True)` functions beside their sources otherwise, and already on import it creates and removes a file in each such `__pycache__` to see whether it may |
| `MPLCONFIGDIR`, `MPLBACKEND=Agg` | matplotlib's font cache, and no display |
| `PYTHONPYCACHEPREFIX` | bytecode is cached there: with a prefix Python reads and writes no `__pycache__` beside a source |
| `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1` | the interpreter writes no bytecode while it starts; the worker turns the writing on itself once it has checked the prefix, below |

The worker sets the same from inside before it imports anything of optiland (`lvrtc_optiland/hygiene.py`), so one
started by hand is as careful. A cache that would lie inside the directory optiland is imported from, or inside
the interpreter's virtual environment, is refused before a directory is made and before numba is imported: a
`cacheDir` that points there makes the engine unavailable and writes nothing. After the import the worker checks
that numba caches where it was told, that its JIT is on, and that optiland computes with numpy in float64. In
every such case it refuses `hello` with the reason. The JIT stays on: optiland is compared as it runs, and numba's
own `NUMBA_DISABLE_JIT` is not obeyed. The worker's standard output is reserved for replies at the level of the
file descriptor before optiland is imported, so a warning of numpy or a `print` in a library is a line of the log.

**One worker runs without the JIT, and says so**: the one started with `LVRTC_OPTILAND_JIT=off`, a switch of the
worker's own that nothing in the comparator sets but the test that compares the two. Its descriptor states
`jit: false`, which is part of the fingerprint, so its answers are another engine's to the result store and are
never found there in place of optiland's as it runs. Any other word than `on` and `off` refuses `hello`.

**Bytecode is cached, under the cache directory and nowhere else.** The stdio transport starts every worker with
`PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`, and a definition can replace a variable of the transport but not remove one. So the
switch is the worker's: `hygiene.prepare` first refuses a prefix that lies inside optiland, then names it
(`sys.pycache_prefix`), and only then lets this interpreter write (`sys.dont_write_bytecode = False`). What is
imported after that, which is numpy, scipy, numba, matplotlib, vtk and optiland, is compiled once and read from
the cache on every later start; what was imported before, the worker's own first modules, is written nowhere. A
process the worker starts is still told not to write.

**Starting takes time.** `hello` is answered once optiland is imported: about 3 s on this machine with warm
caches, and about 18 s the first time, when matplotlib builds its font cache and 2900 modules are compiled and
their bytecode written. Until Stage 2.2 no bytecode was kept and a warm start took 6 s, half of it compiling. The
engine's `hello` may take three minutes (`OPTILAND_TIMEOUTS`) where every other engine's may take 30 s.

**What the JIT costs and saves**, measured at optiland `4e893f53` on a synthetic singlet of two spherical surfaces
with 4096 rays given to `surfaces.trace`: the first trace of a process takes 0.9 s on an empty numba cache, while
the conic intersection is compiled, and 0.23 s once it is cached; every later one 0.8 ms. With the JIT off a
trace takes 20 ms, the first like the rest. One `FFTMTF` of that singlet on the axis (128 rays across the pupil)
takes 17 ms with the JIT on and 31 ms with it off. The landing points are the same bits either way. So are the
answers of a whole run: traced once with the JIT and once without, all 378 ray sets of the two suites come back
as the same bytes, every array of every answer, and no figure of any pair of R2 moves. Without the JIT the
benchmark's 216 sets take the worker 20 s in place of 5 s.

**When it cannot be used** the engine is unavailable and every other engine carries on:

| Code | When | The message says |
|---|---|---|
| `not-configured` | `engines.optiland.python` is `null` | to set it in `lvrtc.local.json`, or `LVRTC_OPTILAND_PYTHON` |
| `spawn-failed` | the interpreter it names is not on this machine | its path, and the same |
| `hello-failed` | the interpreter cannot import optiland, numpy, scipy or numba; or optiland is not in the state the worker computes in; or `cacheDir` lies inside the optiland checkout or its environment | the interpreter and Python's own error, and for an import that failed the same |

**Who it is.** The fingerprint is a hash over the commit and dirty flag of the optiland checkout, a hash of the
package's Python sources, the versions of Python, numpy, scipy and numba, and whether the JIT is on; the
distribution's version string is no part of it, because it ends in the day of the install; the version the engine
states is that string without the day (`0.6.2.post117+g4e893f53`), so that no manifest or report holds a date. The
worker's own sources are not the engine: they are the adapter revision, a hash of `workers/python/lvrtc_optiland`
and the kit, and the result store keys an answer by both ([contract](../contract/CONTRACT.md#engine-descriptor)).
`lvrtc doctor` prints all of it, and `python -m lvrtc_optiland --identity` prints the identity as one line of JSON.

```bash
npm run test:optiland
```

Runs the tests against the real optiland (`test/integration/optiland`), with the interpreter of the configuration:
the Python tests of the worker (`workers/python/tests/optiland`, none skipped: the builder on every shape and
mapping, the mistakes it must catch, optiland's first-order data held to values derived by hand, and its rays
held to closed forms and to a trace in 60 digits), the conformance kit, a run in which R0 to R3 are answered and
the conformance rung `unsupported`, `lvrtc doctor`, and a
recursive snapshot of the optiland checkout and its environment (path, size and modification time of every file
and directory) taken before the first test and after a cold start on an empty cache directory, with the JIT
compiling and the bytecode being written: nothing may differ. `r0.test.ts`, `r1.test.ts` and `r2.test.ts` run
rungs R0, R1 and R2 through the commands: the contract's cases and systems made for the rung against `ref`, which
need optiland only, and the benchmark and feature suites on `lv`, `ref` and `optiland`, which need LensVisualizer
too. `r1.test.ts` also asks the three engines in its own process, without ray sets: at every focus station
LensVisualizer certifies, and optiland against `ref` over every lens of the catalog that exports, a zoom at both
ends. `r2.test.ts` holds every pair of a suite to `PASS` under policy version 11, with no ray stopped by one
engine and passed by another, and optiland to its measured bounds against `ref`; it runs each suite a
second time with optiland without the JIT, and counts the worker processes a run starts, which is one.
`r3.test.ts` runs each suite on all four rungs, as Phase 2 states its benchmark, holds every pair of R3 to `PASS`
and optiland's paths to 1e-7 waves of `ref`'s, to the last surface, to the image plane and relative to
the chief ray; a request of its own gives the contract's cases a chief ray, which their probe lattices lack, and
another states the same rays with directions 4.5e-13 longer and holds each engine to the same paths.
`stations.test.ts` holds the rays of every focus station LensVisualizer certifies, which diverge from an object
point, to both rungs. LV and `ref` must fail no ray; an optiland failure is allowed only on an asphere where both
block that same ray at that same surface, with each ray named in a diagnostic (the Olympus focus-station case
in [gotchas.md](gotchas.md)). What the three share is `traced.ts`. The Python tests of the path are `test_path.py`:
what optiland's sum is made of, and closed forms of a plate, of the two Cartesian conics, of the aplanatic points of a
sphere, of a lens of two spheres traced by hand and of a step backwards; and what optiland makes of a direction
that is no unit vector, in its steps and in its refraction. Each test skips with the reason when
optiland, or LensVisualizer where it is needed, is not configured or cannot be used.

## Phase 2: R0 to R3 on three engines

Historically, Phase 2 closed with the two suites on `lv`, `ref` and `optiland` through rungs R0 to R3, at the
reference line and the five photopic lines, every gated pair `PASS` or `FLOOR`. The measurement below is of
`c05a2ab7` under policy version 5; [Stage 3.12](#measured-at-stage-312) supersedes its floor verdicts.

```bash
node bin/lvrtc.mjs run suites/benchmark.json --engines lv,ref,optiland --rungs r0,r1,r2,r3
```

Has each of the three engines build every case, give its first-order data and trace the rays LensVisualizer
launches; `lvrtc compare benchmark` then judges every two of them on every rung, and exits with 1 if a pair
fails. The benchmark takes 28 s and the feature suite (`suites/features.json`) 19 s.

Measured at optiland `4e893f53` and LensVisualizer `c05a2ab7` (engine closure `78215d72`, 151 files), and
committed as baselines under policy version 5, which gives these suites the verdicts version 4 gave them
([Baselines](#baselines)). Pairs of two engines, as each rung judges them:

| | Runs, ray sets, rays that every engine lands | R0 | R1 | R2 | R3 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `benchmark` | 24 runs, 216 ray sets, 137 596 of 235 812 rays | 72 `PASS` | 72 `PASS` | 648 `PASS` | 648 `PASS` |
| `features` | 18 runs, 162 ray sets, 104 846 of 185 522 rays | 54 `PASS` | 54 `PASS` | 482 `PASS`, 4 `FLOOR` | 482 `PASS`, 4 `FLOOR` |
| the 24 focus stations LensVisualizer certifies, on the reference line | 24 runs, 72 ray sets, 50 357 of 100 004 rays | 72 `PASS` | 72 `PASS` | 214 `PASS`, 2 `FLOOR` | 216 `PASS` |

`lvrtc compare` counts a pair in both of its modes and says 2400 `PASS` for the benchmark, 1784 `PASS` and 16
`FLOOR` for the feature suite, and 956 and 4 for the stations: no `FAIL`, no `ERROR`, nothing `UNSUPPORTED` and
nothing `BLOCKED`. No ray is stopped by one engine and passed by another, none lies in a rim band, and no engine
fails one. The largest figure of each kind, of optiland against `ref` and, after the stroke, of `lv` against `ref`
(`lv` against optiland is the same to two digits):

| Rung | Figure, and its gate | `benchmark` | `features` | focus stations |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R0 | sag on the scale of its rounding, 1e-12; every other value of the built system is equal | 4.5e-16 / 4.0e-16 | 3.3e-16 / 2.7e-16 | 6.0e-16 / 3.8e-16 |
| R1 | a value that is no pupil's, 1e-9 mm | 1.8e-12 / 1.6e-12 | 5.0e-14 / 5.3e-14 | 3.3e-13 / 3.8e-13 |
| R1 | a pupil's position, 1e-9 mm | 1.0e-12 / 3.4e-13 | 9.9e-14 / 8.5e-14 | 7.1e-14 / 2.0e-13 |
| R1 | a pupil's radius, 1e-9 mm | 5.7e-14 / 4.3e-14 | 9.8e-15 / 7.1e-15 | 4.6e-14 / 3.9e-14 |
| R2 | a hit on a surface, 1e-8 mm | 1.2e-12 / 6.8e-9 | 1.1e-12 / 2.3e-9 | 4.5e-12 / 1.12e-8 |
| R2 | the exit direction, 1e-9 | 2.1e-14 / 3.1e-10 | 1.3e-14 / 2.1e-10 | 6.8e-14 / 1.4e-10 |
| R2 | the landing, 1e-8 mm | 8.9e-13 / 9.1e-9 | 5.9e-13 / 1.10e-8 | 4.6e-12 / 1.14e-8 |
| R3 | the path to the last surface, 2e-5 waves | 2.7e-9 / 6.0e-6 | 3.0e-9 / 3.8e-6 | 3.9e-9 / 8.1e-6 |
| R3 | the path to the image plane, 2e-5 waves | 2.7e-9 / 5.3e-6 | 3.1e-9 / 2.07e-5 | 3.0e-9 / 8.3e-6 |
| R3 | the path relative to the chief ray's, 2e-5 waves | 2.7e-9 / 5.5e-6 | 3.5e-9 / 2.28e-5 | 3.3e-9 / 5.9e-6 |

- **optiland and `ref`** share no code and agree on every traced ray to a two-thousandth of each gate of R2 and
  R3 or better: inside what the floor rule asks of a witness (1e-10 mm, 1e-12, 1e-7 waves) by a factor of 22 in a
  hit, 14 in the exit direction and 25 in a path, each at its worst, which is a focus station's. What they differ
  by is optiland's, by the 60-digit trace of the rays that set each figure.
- **The floors are LensVisualizer's**: the Hologon of the feature suite (`stop-inside-element`) at its full field
  at 470 nm and 510 nm, in R2 by its landing and in R3 by its path to the image plane, against `ref` and against
  optiland alike; and one station, `fujifilm-gf80-f17` at its closest focus and full field, in R2 by a hit
  1.12e-8 mm off. Each is its intersection tolerance of 1e-9 mm behind a steep surface, a figure a tighter
  tolerance in LensVisualizer would turn into a `PASS` ([docs/gotchas.md](gotchas.md)).
- **What these tables do not say** is in the sections above and in [docs/gotchas.md](gotchas.md#optiland): on
  some lenses outside the suites optiland loses rays or carries them where the case has no surface, which fails
  its pairs in R2, and on four it is far enough from `ref` to withhold a floor from LensVisualizer, in R2 and in
  R3 until policy version 5 changed what a witness may withhold. The current record, rewritten at Stage 3.12, is
  [reports/benchmark/rays.md](../reports/benchmark/rays.md) and
  [reports/features/rays.md](../reports/features/rays.md), each rendered from its baseline.

The rungs are exercised by `npm run test:optiland`: `r0.test.ts` to `r3.test.ts` and `stations.test.ts`
in `test/integration/optiland`. They now require `PASS` under policy version 11; the figures above are history.
`r3.test.ts` runs the command above on each suite, all four rungs at once, and
`stations.test.ts` the two rungs of traced rays.

## Baselines

A baseline is the committed record of one compared run of a suite: `baselines/<suite>.json`
([the contract's `baseline`](../contract/CONTRACT.md#baseline)). For every run **as it was run** (a zoom at each
end), every rung and every pair of engines it holds the verdict, each metric with its worst value and where it
occurs, how the rays of each engine ended and the rim band's count, and what all of that is of: the content hash
of the run's case, each engine's id, fingerprint and adapter revision with the commit it states, and the policy's
version and hash. It holds no ray, no prescription, no time and no path, and is canonical JSON: the same run gives
the same bytes anywhere.

**To write the two that are committed**, on the checkouts of `lvrtc.config.json`:

```bash
node bin/lvrtc.mjs run suites/benchmark.json --engines lv,ref,optiland --rungs r0,r1,r2,r3,r4
node bin/lvrtc.mjs compare benchmark
node bin/lvrtc.mjs baseline write benchmark
node bin/lvrtc.mjs run suites/features.json --engines lv,ref,optiland --rungs r0,r1,r2,r3,r4
node bin/lvrtc.mjs compare features
node bin/lvrtc.mjs baseline write features
```

`lvrtc baseline write <suite name | run directory> [--root <dir>]` asks no engine anything. It writes
`<root>/baselines/<suite>.json` and the report rendered from it, `<root>/reports/<suite>/rays.md` and `rays.json`:
the inputs (engines with fingerprints and what they were taken at, the policy, every run with its case hash), per
rung the pairs of engines by verdict, the support matrix, the worst of every metric of every pair with its run and
place, and a row per run with each pair's verdict, the judged metric that is largest against its tolerance, and
the ray counts. It refuses (exit 2) a run with an engine that could not be used, a run that was not started, a
LensVisualizer that changed during the run, and comparisons of another manifest or policy; and (exit 1) a run with
a pair that is `FAIL` or `ERROR`: a baseline records what the engines agreed on.

`lvrtc baseline check <suite name | suite.json> [--root <dir>] [--json]` needs the engines. A suite name is
`<root>/suites/<name>.json`. It runs the suite again on the baseline's engines and rungs into the configured runs
directory, where the result store answers whatever has not changed, compares it and writes `comparisons.json` as
`lvrtc compare` does, and sets every record of the baseline (a pair of engines in one rung of one run) against the
same record of that run:

| State | When | What to do |
|---|---|---|
| `OK` | the case hash, both engines' fingerprints and adapter revisions and the policy are the baseline's, and the record is what it was | nothing |
| `STALE(case)` | LensVisualizer now exports another case for the run: a lens file was edited | see the outcome |
| `STALE(engine)` | the fingerprint or the adapter revision of one of the two engines is another | see the outcome |
| `STALE(policy)` | the policy's hash is another | see the outcome |
| `REFRESHABLE` | stale, and the verdicts are the same and no judged metric moved by more than its tolerance | `lvrtc baseline write <suite>`, then commit `baselines/` and `reports/` |
| `DRIFT` | stale or not, a verdict changed or a judged metric moved by more than its rung's tolerance | find the cause first (`lvrtc report <suite>`, [docs/gotchas.md](gotchas.md)); only then write |
| `NEW`, `GONE` | the suite as it runs has a record the baseline lacks, or lacks one it has | `lvrtc baseline write <suite>` once the suite is what is wanted |

Every record that is not `OK` is a line, then the counts, then what to do for each state present. Exit code: 1
when a record is `DRIFT`, when a pair is `FAIL` or `ERROR` today, and when an engine could not be used or
LensVisualizer changed under the run; 0 otherwise, stale or not; 2 when nothing was checked (no suite file, no
baseline). The check leaves the run it made in the runs directory, so `baseline write` after a `REFRESHABLE`
needs no second run. A baseline is of one selection of engines and rungs, the same for every run of the suite;
a rung that is about engines of its own is checked on those, and they are named for no other rung.

`lvrtc verify [--root <dir>] [--write]` is hermetic and part of `npm run check`: it reads no engine, and passes
with `LVRTC_LV_PATH` and `LVRTC_OPTILAND_PYTHON` pointing nowhere. Every `baselines/*.json` must be a baseline by
its schema and its rules, be the canonical text of its content (an edit by hand is not), be named after its suite
and be judged by the policy at hand; `reports/<suite>/rays.md` and `rays.json` must be, byte for byte, what the
baseline renders; no such report may be without its baseline. `--write` writes the reports anew from the baselines
(after a change to the renderer) and never writes a baseline. **It cannot see `STALE`**: a case or an engine that
has changed since needs the engines, and is what `baseline check` says.

**When to rewrite.** After a change to the policy (verify fails until the baselines are of the new one), to `ref`
or to an adapter (`STALE(engine)`), after LensVisualizer's engine files or the lenses of a suite change, and after
a suite changes. `npm run test:optiland` runs `baseline check` on both suites and holds every record that is not
stale to `OK`; a stale record is reported there and fails nothing.

**Current, Stage 3.12.** All four baselines are of policy version 11 and LensVisualizer `ab0fd4cf` (closure
`94778d3d`, 175 files), with the census rewritten too. The saved baseline checks report every record `OK`;
[the measurement and verdict counts](#measured-at-stage-312) distinguish baseline records from request pairs.
The paragraphs below retain the earlier baseline deliveries.

**At Stage 3.2 (history)** the baselines were of policy version 6 and of LensVisualizer `33ebdb30` (the same engine
closure, `78215d72`). The benchmark's holds the suite's 96 runs
([the benchmark in four conditions](#the-benchmark-in-four-conditions)): 1152 records, all `PASS`. The 288 records
of the 24 runs it held before, and the 216 of the feature suite (212 `PASS`, 4 `FLOOR`), are what they were in
every verdict, figure and count; 864 records are new, those of the 72 runs at f/8 and at LensVisualizer's best
focus. From an empty store `lvrtc baseline check benchmark`, which is the suite's run on three engines and its
comparison, takes about two and a half minutes, and the feature suite's 24 s (measured within
`npm run test:optiland`).

**At Stage 3.3 (history)** the baselines were of policy version 7 and gained R4 beside R0 to R3: 1440 records of the
benchmark (96 runs, five rungs, three pairs of engines), all `PASS`, and 270 of the feature suite (266 `PASS`,
4 `FLOOR`). The 1152 and 216 records of R0 to R3 are what they were in every verdict, figure and count; 288 and 54
are new, those of R4, at the same LensVisualizer `33ebdb30` and the same fingerprints and adapter revisions. R4 is
in the baselines because they take it as they are: its requests are those of R2 and R3, so a check traces nothing
more for it, `reports/<suite>/rays.md` states its worst figure for every pair of engines with the run, the field
and the frequency, and `npm run test:optiland` holds it on all 96 runs without a second trace. On a warm store it
adds about 45 s to `lvrtc baseline check benchmark`: the recipe of each run, and the estimator.

**The rays baselines hold R0 to R4 and no other rung**, and the commands above name those for that reason: the
MTF rungs are in baselines of their own ([below](#mtf-baselines)). A rays baseline can hold `r4f` all the same
(`test/cli/fidelityLadder.test.ts`): `baseline write` without `--mtf` records every rung of the run it is given.

### MTF baselines

Stage 3.8. The MTF rungs of a suite are in a second baseline, `baselines/<suite>.mtf.json`, with its own report,
`reports/<suite>/mtf.md` and `mtf.json`. It is a `baseline` like the other and is keyed the same way, on runs as
run by name and case hash; `lvrtc baseline write`, `baseline check` and `verify` take it with `--mtf`.

| | Rays baseline | MTF baseline |
|---|---|---|
| File | `baselines/<suite>.json` | `baselines/<suite>.mtf.json` |
| Reports | `reports/<suite>/rays.md`, `rays.json` | `reports/<suite>/mtf.md`, `mtf.json` |
| Rungs | every rung of the run it is written from: R0 to R4 as committed | `r4`, `r4f`, `r6a` (gated) and `r5`, `r5g`, `r6b` (recorded), those of them the run has |
| Contract | 1.0 | 1.1: each metric's `measured`, a rung's `steps`, `bands` and `sets` |
| From an empty store | about 3 minutes for the benchmark | about an hour and 15 GB for the benchmark |

**Why a file of its own.** The rays baseline has its own report and verification; adding MTF baselines at
Stage 3.8 left those byte-identical then. Stage 3.12 rewrote both kinds. A check of the rays rungs stays a matter
of minutes, where the MTF rungs cost an hour from an empty store
and need optiland for a third of it. And an MTF baseline holds more than verdicts and worst figures: a recorded
rung has no gate, so what is worth keeping of it is the table. R4 is in both: its requests are R2's and R3's, so
it costs the rays baseline nothing, and the MTF report is rendered from the MTF baseline alone and has to show it.

**What it holds beyond a rays baseline** (the members of contract 1.1,
[contract/CONTRACT.md](../contract/CONTRACT.md#baseline)):

- each metric of a pair with the number of requests it was `measured` in. R6a judges a field only where its
  lattice is an arbiter: `waveMtf.maxAbs` is measured in the judged fields, `waveMtf.flagged` in the others, and a
  flagged field is `PASS` in the verdict counts. The report therefore states "208 of 288" beside the figure, and
  the counts of judged fields are a figure a check sees move;
- the later steps of an engine (`fft512`, `geo512`): how many requests were asked again, and how they ended. They
  are no part of `support`, which is of an engine's first answers;
- for R5 and R5g, every request as it was compared: what each engine's answer records (every field's status,
  sampling, chief-ray landing and both curves at all eleven frequencies) and each pair's verdict, reason and
  figures, with the attention bands of the policy. The class and the reason code of every field are derived from
  these as the comparator derives them (`classifyNativeField`), so a field without a curve is in the table with
  its reason and not only in a pair's sentence.

It holds no ray, no prescription, no time and no path.

**The report** (`src/baseline/mtfReport.ts`, rendered from the baseline alone): the inputs; per rung the pairs by
verdict and the support; for each rung the worst of every metric with its run, field, cut and frequency and the
requests it was measured in; for a gated rung a row per run; for R5 and R5g the table of the three estimates, a
row per run, field and cut at 10, 30 and 50 cycles/mm with each engine's own flag (ok, unconverged, no curve), the
step optiland's row is of, the class and reason of the field, the gravest status of the three frequencies and the
verdict over all eleven; the requests without a table by reason; the later steps; every request marked
`ATTENTION` with each figure above its band; the fixed block **Not covered**; and how the tables are read. Its
wording is held by the lint of `src/report/wording.ts`.

**To write the two that are committed**, after the run of the MTF rungs:

```bash
node bin/lvrtc.mjs run suites/benchmark.json --engines lv,ref,optiland --rungs r4,r4f,r5,r5g,r6a,r6b
node bin/lvrtc.mjs compare benchmark
node bin/lvrtc.mjs baseline write benchmark --mtf
node bin/lvrtc.mjs run suites/features.json --engines lv,ref,optiland --rungs r4,r4f,r6a
node bin/lvrtc.mjs compare features
node bin/lvrtc.mjs baseline write features --mtf
```

The feature suite's holds the gated rungs only; the recorded rungs are of the benchmark. A run directory is of
the last run of its suite, so the rays rungs and the MTF rungs are run, compared and written one after the other;
the result store keeps the answers of both.

**`lvrtc baseline check <suite> --mtf`** runs the rungs of the MTF baseline again on its engines and sets every
record against the run, as for a rays baseline. Two things are added, for either kind of baseline:

| State | When | What to do |
|---|---|---|
| `MOVED` | a record of a rung that is only recorded: a verdict changed (`RECORDED` to `ATTENTION`, a pair that lost its curve), or a figure with an attention band moved by more than the band | read what moved; `lvrtc baseline write <suite> --mtf` records it. It fails nothing: a recorded rung has no gate |
| `DRIFT` | as before, and only of a gated rung | find the cause first |

and after the counts, **every figure that is not what the baseline has**, however little it moved: for each rung,
pair and metric, in how many records, the largest change and the run it is in (`changes` of a record and of the
whole check with `--json`). A gate says whether a change matters; this list says what changed, which is the
record wanted when an engine is changed on purpose. The values a kept request records are not set against each
other one by one: the figures of each pair are their maxima, and the tables of the two reports can be compared
once the baseline is written anew (`git diff reports/<suite>/mtf.md`). A pair that is `ERROR` today fails the
check in a recorded rung as in a gated one.

**After a change to LensVisualizer's engine files**, to see what moved and to record it:

```bash
node bin/lvrtc.mjs baseline check benchmark            # rays: R0 to R4, minutes
node bin/lvrtc.mjs baseline check benchmark --mtf      # MTF: lv's answers anew; optiland's own are the store's while the case is the same
node bin/lvrtc.mjs baseline check features
node bin/lvrtc.mjs baseline check features --mtf
# then, where nothing is DRIFT, FAIL or ERROR, each run directory being of the check that was last:
node bin/lvrtc.mjs baseline check benchmark && node bin/lvrtc.mjs baseline write benchmark
node bin/lvrtc.mjs baseline check benchmark --mtf && node bin/lvrtc.mjs baseline write benchmark --mtf
```

A check leaves its run in the runs directory, and `baseline write` reads the run that is there: write a baseline
directly after its own check, as above. Written after the check of the other kind it would lose its rungs, so
`baseline write` refuses (exit 2) a run that lacks a rung the baseline at hand has, unless `--replace` is given. A run at LensVisualizer's best
focus has another case when the focus search moves in its last digit, and optiland then computes its own MTF of
that case again.

## Workers over stdio

A `stdio` engine is a worker process. The comparator writes one protocol message per line to its standard input
and reads one reply per line from its standard output; the worker's standard error is its log, read all the time
and kept as a tail that is quoted when the worker fails.

```json
{
  "engines": {
    "fake-py": {
      "transport": "stdio",
      "command": ["${python}", "-m", "lvrtc_worker_kit.fake_engine"],
      "options": { "id": "fake-py" },
      "env": { "PYTHONPATH": "${root}/workers/python" }
    }
  }
}
```

- **The command** is started once, without a shell, with the configuration root as its working directory. Its
  first word is a bare command name, looked up on `PATH`, or a path resolved against the root; the other words are
  passed as written.
- **Placeholders.** In `command` and in the values of `env`, `${root}` is the configuration root and `${python}`
  the configuration value `python`. Nothing else is replaced, and any other `${...}` is an error naming the file.
- **The environment** is built, not inherited: `PATH`, `HOME` and the few other variables a program needs to
  start; `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1` and `PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1`; `LVRTC_ENGINE_OPTIONS`, the definition's `options`
  as JSON; then the definition's `env`, which may replace any of them. Nothing is guessed: a Python worker gets
  its `PYTHONPATH` from `env`, as above.
- **One message at a time** per worker. A worker that does not reply in time is killed and the call reported as a
  timeout; one that exits, writes a line that is not JSON, writes a line that is too long, or answers another
  message than the one asked is reported with a code that says which (`exited`, `not-json`, `oversized-line`,
  `unexpected-reply`). A worker that has gone stays gone for the rest of the run.
- **Closing** sends `shutdown`, closes the worker's input, waits two seconds and then kills it. Only the worker
  is waited for: a process the worker started may keep the worker's output open after it, and the comparator stops
  reading a second after the worker has ended.

`lvrtc engine conformance <id> [--root <dir>] [--json]` checks an engine, configured or built in, against the
contract, over whatever transport reaches it: `hello` gives a valid descriptor of this contract and of the id it
was asked under; an unknown quantity is answered `unsupported`, not with an error; a malformed `run` is refused
with `ok: false`; ids are echoed; if the engine offers `selftest.echo`, the contract's examples are answered byte
for byte (a NaN with a payload, −0, the infinities, subnormals, the largest double, an empty array, a 2-D array,
scaling and summing); a repeated request gets an equal result if the descriptor says the engine is deterministic;
and `shutdown` is answered with an empty object, after which a worker process ends by itself with exit code 0.
Each check is printed `PASS`, `FAIL` or `SKIPPED` with a reason. The exit code is 0 when nothing failed, 1 when a
check did, and 2 for an id that is neither a configured nor a built-in engine.

## The Python worker kit

`workers/python/lvrtc_worker_kit` is what a Python worker needs to speak the protocol. It imports nothing outside
the standard library, writes nothing to disk and runs on Python 3.10 and later.

| Module | Is |
|---|---|
| `ndarray` | the NdArray wire form: bit-exact, independent of the host's byte order |
| `validate` | the contract's schema validator, a port of `src/contract/validate.ts` that reads `contract/schema/v1` |
| `protocol` | the NDJSON loop around an engine object with `describe()` and `run(request, case)` |
| `fake_engine` | the fake engine as a worker: `python -m lvrtc_worker_kit.fake_engine [--id ID] [--bias X] [--no-quantities] [--fingerprint F]` |

The loop answers every line with exactly one line and never crashes on one: a line that is not JSON, an unknown
method and a request that is not valid by the contract are answered
`{ "ok": false, "error": { "code", "message" } }`. An exception in an engine's `run` is the engine's failure on
that request and no fault of the protocol: it is answered `ok: true` with a result of status `error` and the code
`engine-failure`, as the engines of the comparator's own process answer it. A worker echoes the ids it is given
and never recomputes them.

Standard output carries replies only, and that is kept at the level of the file descriptor: `protect_stdout()`
duplicates descriptor 1 for the replies and points descriptor 1 itself at the standard error, so a `print`, a
warning, a C library and a process the worker starts all write to the log. `serve_stdio` does it for a worker
that has not; a worker whose engine may write while it is imported calls it first.

The Python fake engine and the TypeScript one are interchangeable: for one request they give byte-identical
results. The kit's tests are `unittest`, in `workers/python/tests/kit`, and run the validator against the same
fixture corpus as the TypeScript tests; those of the optiland worker are beside them, in
`workers/python/tests/optiland`, and run on a fake optiland (`test/fixtures/fake-optiland`) under any Python:

```bash
python3 -m unittest discover -s workers/python/tests/kit -t workers/python
python3 -m unittest discover -s workers/python/tests/optiland -t workers/python
```

## Rung R6c: FFT and raw wavefront beside the two LV estimates

Run only where named: `lvrtc run <suite> --engines lv,ref,optiland --rungs r6c`. The rung supplies its four engines
`lv`, `wave`, `optiland`, `optiland-wave`, regardless of the run's engines. It uses R5's request, fields and recipe
angles, frequencies thinned to eleven, on the plane of the case. Stage 4.3 adds several-line requests below.
No catalog sweep or baseline write is part of Stage 4.1.

The worker's new `wavefront.native` version 1 returns `Wavefront.get_data`, not an optiland OTF. Its even midpoint
grids (128 and 256 across) lie strictly inside the pupil edge. Each grid, line and field has a fresh verified
optic, robust stop aiming and chief-ray sphere, tilt retained. The raw arrays preserve pupil coordinates in mm,
OPD in vacuum waves, intensity, validity, sphere center/radius and exit pupil. Errors are per-field rows except
FATAL build, memory or import errors; REQUEST_BUDGET_S still bounds a request between fields. The complete wire
shape is in [the contract](../contract/CONTRACT.md#wavefrontnative).

The new adapter `optiland-wave` negotiates and validates those raw answers and applies the comparator's pure
`wavefrontOtf` estimator in TypeScript. It uses radial directions from the sphere points to its center, n times
those transverse components as optical cosines, W=-lambda OPD and intensity as cell flux. These are reference-
sphere directions, not optiland's traced exit directions. The unchanged Hopkins kernel handles the Jacobian and
bilinear shears; the new file changes no operation or adapter revision of `lv`, `ref`, `replay` or `wave`.
Disc and annulus overlap bounds, an affine tilt's complex phase, a defocus sinc with a derived midpoint bound,
whole-number sums of an apodized pupil in a medium, and a translated sphere's unchanged OTF are proved
in `test/estimators/wavefrontOtf.test.ts`. Rim error is first order, not removed by an interior grid.

R6c uses R5's sorting helpers and bands (0.005 on axis, 0.01 off it, chief landing 1e-7 mm), under policy 12. Every
difference is sorted before subtraction: unsupported, data, numerical, method. The table's four values are LV's
sheared estimate, wave OTF on LV's rays, optiland FFT and comparator OTF of optiland wavefront; the last three
separately classified differences are LV, wave and FFT minus the raw-wavefront estimate. The raw estimate is
numerical at more than a quarter wave per cell or a move on doubling beyond the field's band; no curve is an
arbiter of another. There is no automatic 512 follow-up in R6c. Photopic combination is recorded below, and the
cause of Z 50/1.2's FFT axis differences is investigated in [Stage 4.2](z50f12-investigation.md), below.

The `r6c-stand-ins` golden and its MTF-baseline golden are generated by `node test/report/writeGolden.ts` and
linted. MTF baselines can add its detailed records, including all four columns and classes. Existing baselines
and reports were left alone: `lvrtc verify` reports their policy-11 hashes until the owner rewrites them.

### Stage 4.1 measurement (four configurations, no baseline write)

LensVisualizer `ab0fd4cf` (closure `94778d3d`, 175 files); optiland `4e893f53`, dirty checkout as supplied,
fingerprint `bcfbf3916c1103b3…` (source hash `279af5c55d8ebe16…`), numpy float64, JIT on. The two prime lenses were
run wide open at design and at f/8 best axial focus, on the 587.5618 nm reference line, at fractions 0/0.5/1 and
eleven frequencies 0–100 cycles/mm. Sixteen jobs answered ok, no request errors. This is a measurement, not a
baseline: suite, store, manifest, comparisons and rendered run report were under a temporary LVRTC_RUNS_DIR.

Values below are the tangential cut at **30 cycles/mm**. The class/status is **LV minus raw wavefront at this
frequency**; the report also sorts wave-minus-raw and FFT-minus-raw separately. A star marks a column whose own
sampling did not settle, so a difference involving it is numerical and in no band. A dash is no curve.

| Lens | Conditions | Field | lv, sheared | wave, LV rays | optiland FFT | Comparator, optiland wavefront | Class / status of LV minus raw |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|
| Canon EF 135/2L | wide open, design | 0 | 0.945181 | 0.944237 | 0.944610 | 0.941176 | method / RECORDED |
| Canon EF 135/2L | wide open, design | 0.5 | 0.869985 | 0.870361 | 0.868339 | 0.869311 | method / RECORDED |
| Canon EF 135/2L | wide open, design | 1 | 0.714545 | 0.714550 | 0.713050 | 0.712546 | method / RECORDED |
| Nikkor Z 50/1.2 | wide open, design | 0 | 0.171413 | 0.172229* | 0.165705* | 0.151047 | method / ATTENTION |
| Nikkor Z 50/1.2 | wide open, design | 0.5 | 0.406882 | 0.406757* | 0.449498* | 0.398662 | method / RECORDED |
| Nikkor Z 50/1.2 | wide open, design | 1 | 0.575396 | 0.571571* | — | 0.572350* | numerical / SET ASIDE |
| Canon EF 135/2L | f/8, best axial | 0 | 0.827687 | 0.824889 | 0.826333 | 0.825679 | method / RECORDED |
| Canon EF 135/2L | f/8, best axial | 0.5 | 0.797525 | 0.797265 | 0.797447 | 0.796721 | method / RECORDED |
| Canon EF 135/2L | f/8, best axial | 1 | 0.701004 | 0.700675 | 0.700261 | 0.699521 | method / RECORDED |
| Nikkor Z 50/1.2 | f/8, best axial | 0 | 0.828445 | 0.826516 | 0.827340 | 0.826941 | method / RECORDED |
| Nikkor Z 50/1.2 | f/8, best axial | 0.5 | 0.817445 | 0.817127 | 0.817455 | 0.817047 | method / RECORDED |
| Nikkor Z 50/1.2 | f/8, best axial | 1 | 0.758596 | 0.759067 | 0.758760 | 0.757972 | method / RECORDED |

Over all eleven frequencies, both wide-open **LV/raw-wavefront pairs are ATTENTION**; both f/8 best-axial pairs
are RECORDED. On Nikkor wide open, the LV/raw axial difference reaches 0.020366 at 30 cycles/mm; the half-field
0.008219 above is inside its 0.01 band, and full field is set aside because the raw estimate is undersampled.
The wave column is undersampled and not converged at all three fields; the FFT is not converged at the first two
and raises ValueError at full field. Those are numerical/unsupported comparisons, not an inference about which
method is right. No data-class field occurred in these four configurations; synthetic report tests exercise it.
The reference-sphere model, the stop-to-cosine map and the FFT frequency axes have not been attributed by this
stage. [Stage 4.2](z50f12-investigation.md) supplies the Z 50/1.2 attribution; Stage 4.3 supplies photopic combination.

### Stage 4.2: Z 50/1.2 attribution

The [evidence note](z50f12-investigation.md) contains every requested off-axis value at the eleven R6c
frequencies (0:10:100), both cuts, wide open / f/8 and design / best axial focus. It reproduces the
48 native jobs with `lvrtc run` / `compare` under a temporary `LVRTC_RUNS_DIR`, FFT 128/256/512,
wave 64/128/256 and raw-wavefront 128/256/512; a separate bound LV sheared call checks fixed
32/64/128/256 cells. No LV prescription, baseline, report or policy change is part of this stage.

**Class: method**, established on the settled best-focus half field. At T 100 cycles/mm the FFT
is **0.301839 → 0.301717** on 256→512, while LV's adaptive sheared estimate is **0.203097**,
its own fixed-grid estimate at 256 is **0.202370**, wave at 256 is **0.202284** and the comparator's
OTF of optiland's raw wavefront at 512 is **0.204518**. This persistent roughly 0.10 difference
is optiland's stop-grid FFT / full-rim affine cosine calibration on a non-affine vignetted pupil,
not a missing declared comparator transform. All those finest best-half-field estimates stand by
their curves. Fine sampling's small move does not establish exact physical accuracy.

The original research's **0.043** is reproduced at design half field, T 30: LV **0.406882** beside
FFT 256 **0.449498** (difference **0.042615**); FFT 512 is **0.448634**. This design-plane FFT
field remains numerically flagged (S drives its final maximum move of 0.025132). Wide-open full-field
FFT remains unsupported at every grid; wave remains undersampled there through 256. The note keeps
those earlier classes rather than treating an unflagged modulus at one frequency as a settled field.
At f/8 every field settles, and each method's last maximum tangential move is at most 0.00046.

The measured full-rim tangential bandwidth is unchanged on doubling; the actual lit pupil's extents
and local pitches are measured separately. A clipped mask does not by itself require an axis correction:
affine coordinate pitch is still the full grid's pitch. The method's affine approximation is the limit
here. The [upstream question draft](upstream/optiland-vignetted-pupil-frequency-calibration.md) asks about
that supported domain and a possible validity diagnostic. It asserts no upstream implementation defect
and has not been filed. Synthetic real-calibrator and clipped-mask tests pin this finding; the LV
integration test holds the new binding call to the existing product. Policy **12** and all bands remain unchanged.


### Stage 4.3: photopic complex wavefront sum (two configurations)

Measured on Stage 4.2 `49a5360` plus Stage 4.3, under policy **13**, hash
`8149c3b1aa865238b62cfc65c9c3656b047ea4f54caeecaac95d4bf8edf985db`. LV remains `ab0fd4cf`
(engine closure `94778d3d`, 175 files), optiland remains `4e893f53` with its supplied dirty status,
source hash `279af5c55d8ebe16…`, fingerprint `bcfbf3916c1103b3…`, numpy float64 and JIT on.
No catalog sweep, baseline write or committed report rewrite was performed.

`optiland-wave` now makes **one raw wavefront request per case line**, with every field and both midpoint
grids 128/256 in that request. Each line's complex `wavefrontOtf` is evaluated **directly at the requested
frequencies** (0:10:100 cycles/mm here): no frequency interpolation or interpolation error is introduced.
The pure `polychromaticWavefrontOtf` multiplies by exp(-2 pi i nu (chief_l-chief_0)), x sagittal / y tangential,
then by case weight times transmitted cell flux, adds compensated complex numbers, and divides by total
weighted flux before taking the modulus. The common origin is **line 0's chief landing**, as the plan and
case contract prescribe. Both pupils and chiefs stay in optiland's declared y-mirrored frame. A missing line
makes the field unavailable and names that line. No line is dropped, and no FFT moduli are combined.

LV's spectrum uses one common chief launch and wave uses the reference-line flux centroid as its common origin.
An origin shared by every line changes a spectrum's phase, not its modulus; neither existing adapter's
operations or reference convention changed. The raw model still uses radial reference-sphere optical cosines,
not aberrated exit-ray directions. Each line keeps its own chief-relative phase-step check; the sum on each
of the two grids supplies spectral convergence. The analytic square/disc tests derive the declared complex
sum with rounding/rim bounds. Without chief re-referencing, the synthetic sum differs by more than **0.1**;
omitting case weights or flux changes it by more than **0.05**. All three temporary mutation copies make
those proofs red. This is a sum/convention proof, not a check of physical spectral weights.

Both cases use LV's **photopic best-axial** search, asked through `lvBestAxialFocus` at the run's stop, lines
and cap. The recipe records the returned shift separately from the rounded vertex-plus-shift difference:

| Run | LV returned best shift [mm] | Case shift after vertex rounding [mm] | Case imageZ [mm] |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| `canon-ef-135-f2l-usm-best-photopic` | -0.02802958409734814 | -0.028029584097339466 | 155.09197041590267 |
| `nikkor-z50f12-best-photopic` | -0.05152770575191982 | -0.05152770575190857 | 163.2544722942481 |

All methods were asked about that same case plane with `focus: design`; each computed answer states
`appliedShiftMm: 0`. The raw adapter checks every line's sphere center against that plane to the worker's
1e-9 mm tolerance. LV's answer itself requires its repeated best-axial search to give the case plane to the bit.
The five case lines are **555, 470, 510, 610, 650 nm**, with respective weights
**1, 0.091, 0.503, 0.503, 0.107**. Line 0 is 555 nm. The case ids are
`79b7e9b31278c2f402a92f81eafb628841d74ac037fd6376fecd5eed21970735` (Canon) and
`784a2618eede167340d3e9221b8c297d46bbbaed2e06d55e39c311264f0a4a5f` (Nikon).

Reproduce from this repository; all cases, raw arrays, native curves and rendered reports remain temporary:

```sh
POLY=$(mktemp -d /tmp/lvrtc-photopic.XXXXXX)
node --input-type=module - "$POLY" <<'JS'
import { readFileSync, writeFileSync } from "node:fs";
const suite = JSON.parse(readFileSync("suites/benchmark.json", "utf8"));
const names = ["nikkor-z50f12-best-photopic", "canon-ef-135-f2l-usm-best-photopic"];
suite.name = "stage43-photopic";
suite.defaults.rungs = ["r6c"];
suite.runs = suite.runs.filter(run => names.includes(run.name));
if (suite.runs.length !== 2) throw Error("expected two configurations");
writeFileSync(`${process.argv[2]}/suite.json`, JSON.stringify(suite, null, 2) + "\n");
JS
LVRTC_RUNS_DIR="$POLY/runs" node bin/lvrtc.mjs run "$POLY/suite.json"
LVRTC_RUNS_DIR="$POLY/runs" node bin/lvrtc.mjs compare stage43-photopic
LVRTC_RUNS_DIR="$POLY/runs" node bin/lvrtc.mjs report stage43-photopic
```

The temporary manifest retains the recipe's plane, and `runs/store/<storeKey>.json` retains every requested
frequency. At each entry's `request.spec.frequenciesPerMm` index, decode `result.data.fields[i].sagittal` or
`tangential` with `decodeNdArray` from `src/core/numeric/ndarray.ts`. The following extracts 30 and 100 from
those same saved answers (the report shows its configured subset of frequencies):

```sh
node --input-type=module - "$POLY" <<'JS'
import { readFileSync } from "node:fs";
import { decodeNdArray } from "./src/core/numeric/ndarray.ts";
const root = `${process.argv[2]}/runs`;
const manifest = JSON.parse(readFileSync(`${root}/stage43-photopic/manifest.json`, "utf8"));
for (const job of manifest.jobs) {
  const {request, result} = JSON.parse(readFileSync(`${root}/store/${job.storeKey}.json`, "utf8"));
  if (result.status !== "ok") { console.log(job.run, job.engine, result.status); continue; }
  for (const field of result.data.fields) for (const cut of ["sagittal", "tangential"]) {
    const curve = decodeNdArray(field[cut]).values;
    console.log(job.run, job.engine, field.field, cut, field.status,
      ...[30,100].map(nu => curve[request.spec.frequenciesPerMm.indexOf(nu)]));
  }
}
JS
```

**Coverage and classes.** Eight jobs completed: six ok envelopes, two expected several-line FFT unsupported,
no errors. Both cuts and fields 0/0.5/1 are retained. `lv` uses its adaptive product grid (Canon 32 everywhere;
Nikon 32/64/128), `wave` uses the run's default 32/64 pair, and the raw wavefront uses 128/256.
An asterisk below means that engine calls the field unconverged. On 2026-10-22 these runs were re-measured
with `LVRTC_RUNS_DIR` under `/tmp/lvrtc-photopic-followup.xMXKNu/runs`, using the commands above and the
owner's two-limit policy 13. Both judged answers record five lines and a selected chief-landing sorting limit
of **1e-3 mm** per field. The report states that limit through its linted wording template. The columns retain
the same native values; their sorting and shown differences below now use the several-line limit.
Differences are taken from full precision values before rounding; `Status` is per row, not the whole pair.
These are independent method columns, with no arbiter.

The pair's off-axis band figure is therefore **0.010416317** for Canon and **0.013097120** for Nikon.
Nikon full field is reported separately as `mtfFlagged.maxAbs`, never included in that band figure.
The measured chief offsets are inside **1e-3 mm**; at **1e-7 mm** these four fields were data before any
numerical or method difference was considered. LV's shared launch, solved at the d line without a wavelength,
and optiland's line-0 stop-centred chief at 555 nm are different rays. A common reference point preserves
the spectral modulus, so the chief landing only checks that the two answers describe the same field;
it is not evidence that these are one ray's landings or that a tracer is wrong.

Raw wavefront maximum moves on doubling, at fields 0/0.5/1, are **0.000437911 / 0.000532276 / 0.000947870**
(Canon) and **0.000918061 / 0.000473895 / 0.001003483** (Nikon). Its largest per-cell phase steps in waves
are **0.068791 / 0.069935 / 0.113504** and **0.058794 / 0.202363 / 0.557620**, respectively. Thus all Canon
raw fields and Nikon's first two stand by their estimates; Nikon full field is undersampled. Every default
wave field is numerical (Canon undersampled; Nikon on-axis not-converged and off-axis undersampled+not-converged).
The several-line landing check passes first, then numerical sorting takes precedence over method bands.
FFT remains unsupported on every photopic field.

Two one-line answers retain 1e-7 mm; either answer recording several lines selects 1e-3 mm, independent of
the case. R5 and R5g are unchanged. The MTF attention bands, classes, gates, floors, quantity versions and
wire spec do not change. Each answer records the selected `chiefLandingLimitMm` with its wavelengths,
so comparator facts, report facts and baseline reports reproduce the same sorting. The report's
`severalLinesChiefLanding` template names the selected limit; regenerated/linted run/report and MTF
stand-ins cover it. Policy-12 report compatibility constants and branches have been removed. All four
committed baselines still carry policy 12 and await the owner's rewrite; historical report-byte differences
from the removed compatibility wording are also expected until that rewrite. No baseline or reports/ file
was edited, and no commit, push, stash, checkout or reset was performed.

### Stage 4.4: focused state matrix

`suites/states.json` extends the 12 benchmark configurations with the slider stops f/5.6 and f/11
(`aperture.kind: "f-number"`), on reference and photopic lines, at design and LV best-axial planes. It also
asks the benchmark zoom at wide open and `lv-f8-comparison` at both planes/spectra. The f/8 kind retains the
MTF tab's arithmetic; a slider f/8 has different last bits on some lenses. A requested stop outside the slider
range is a coded run problem, never another aperture.

All these runs opt into `lens.stations: "published-zoom"`: primes remain one state; zooms expand to their two
ends plus the coordinates LV's public `publishedZoomStations` publishes, sorted and deduplicated. Unpublished
interpolator knots are excluded. At LV `ab0fd4cf` this is the Z 24-70/4 S at 0, 0.5 and 1; 128 expanded runs.
The wide-open/f8 end states overlap the benchmark deliberately, so one suite contains the complete zoom matrix.

`suites/focus-stations.json` asks `{ "kind": "lv", "key": "*", "stations": "certified-focus" }`.
Only catalog entries declaring V2 focus conjugates are built. Their keyframe and zoom-station addresses are
resolved at run time. All 55 stations across 35 lenses at `ab0fd4cf` become ordinary runs, wide open at the
design plane on the reference line. Of these, 53 export finite cases. The declared close-focus stations of
`olympus-mzuiko-8mm-f18-fisheye-pro` and `sigma-15mm-f14-dg-dn-diagonal-fisheye-art` remain visible runs:
LV's gate refuses each with `unsupported-path` (fisheye projection) and supplies no finite conjugate, so the
exporter records `finite-conjugate-unavailable`. They have no comparison pairs and fail nothing.
No focusT list or prescription is committed. The address-to-slider mapping (`keyframes[keyframe]`, `zoomStation / (count - 1)`) restates LV's
`mtfFiniteConjugate`, with a source canary; Tier 2 compares the expansion with the stations
`test/integration/lv/support.ts` derives and exports one by one, and the optiland stations test runs the committed
suite on R2/R3 and keeps its ray/path assertions.

Both suites state contract `1.3`: minor 3 covers `lens.stations` and policy metric `attentionSeveralLines`.
The policy now states `1.3`, version 14 (the text changed; limits did not). A policy using that member under an
earlier minor is refused by the schema and loader. A suite that uses station selectors and states an
earlier version is refused when it is loaded (`runContractProblems`). The runs the selectors give, and every case,
request, result and manifest are written as before. Baselines with source problems state `1.3`, preserve
`problems` and nullable `caseId`, and give refused runs no rungs; other baselines are written as before.
The benchmark, features and smoke suites keep
`1.0` and their hashes, and the cases of all their 119 runs as run were compared before and after this stage and
are the same, problems included.

The selectors are LV case-source expansions (`CaseSource.expand`), not `expandSuite` matrix operations. They
consume `lens.stations` and produce ordinary runs with explicit coordinates. The suite hash is of the written
selector; baselines key the expanded names and case ids. A selector cannot also state focus or zoom coordinates.
Missing LV and an empty selection remain visible run problems. Other suites keep the ends-only default.

Both suites name only R0-R4, R4f and R6a, with `lv,ref,optiland` (R4f uses lv/replay). The recorded rungs are
outside this delivery. The baseline machinery accepts both suites without special cases; the fake-tree tests
round trip separate rays and MTF baselines, check them and verify their reports under a temporary root.

The owner records the new baselines and reports. Run each baseline's rungs, compare, write immediately, then
check; `baseline check` requires an existing baseline, so it follows the first write. On a subsequent refresh,
check before writing. A suite's run directory always holds its last invocation.

```bash
export LVRTC_RUNS_DIR="$(mktemp -d /tmp/lvrtc-stage44-owner-XXXXXX)"
for S in states focus-stations; do
  node bin/lvrtc.mjs run "suites/$S.json" --rungs r0,r1,r2,r3,r4 &&
  node bin/lvrtc.mjs compare "$S" &&
  node bin/lvrtc.mjs baseline write "$S" &&
  node bin/lvrtc.mjs baseline check "$S"
  node bin/lvrtc.mjs run "suites/$S.json" --rungs r4,r4f,r6a &&
  node bin/lvrtc.mjs compare "$S" &&
  node bin/lvrtc.mjs baseline write "$S" --mtf &&
  node bin/lvrtc.mjs baseline check "$S" --mtf
done
node bin/lvrtc.mjs verify
```

The published-stations import grows LV's closure from 175 to 177 files (`7b756ee6`) at `ab0fd4cf`, which moves
the fingerprint of `lv`, `replay` and `wave`, and the stage's edits of files in the built-in engines' import
closures (`src/contract/runSpec.ts`, `version.ts`, `src/core/suite.ts`, the LV case source) move their adapter
revisions. Existing baselines require the owner's policy-14 refresh: hermetic verify rejects their policy-13 hashes,
and their live checks report `STALE(engine|policy)`.
Refresh them only after inspection, each check immediately followed by its corresponding write.
No gate, existing baseline or reports/ file was changed by the implementer.

Finite-focus coverage limits are retained: the closest Leica Summilux-C 18/T1.4 and tele Panasonic 12-60
stations have no full-field set (`outside-modeled-field`); the closest Samyang 14/2.8 also has no half-field
chief ray. There are 155 available reference-line ray sets from 53 states (four missing field sets).
For R6a's pairwise mode, 132 pairs have a judged wave residual, 327 record it as flagged by the existing phase-step
or grid-doubling checks, and six have no wave estimate because the GF80/1.7 finite station's cosine lattice folds.
These are coverage limits, not newly relaxed gates: PASS is the comparator's verdict on the figures its existing
policy judges, and does not certify every field as a wave arbiter.

## Stage 5.2a — folded systems

The owner selected the 16 keys in `suites/folded.json`: seven production lenses and nine reference systems in
the census list. The suite names lv, ref and optiland, R0–R3, reference line, design plane, wide open, explicit
0 and 0.25 degree fields, and a 16-cell probe lattice. It has no baseline. Its runs, comparisons and the HEAD
compatibility runs are under temporary `/tmp/lvrtc-52-*` directories; `runs/store` is untouched.

Contract 1.4 carries physical surfaces plus fixed visits, both incidence-side policies and both medium references,
annular/opaque windows, and the actual axial image plane. Folded ray requests select definition 2; sequential
requests omit the version member and retain definition 1. R0 compares the physical geometry and the independently
read-back path policy; R2/R3 index hits by visit. Ref's closed proofs cover R/2 and spherical aberration,
paraboloid focus and equal path, Cassegrain EFL, Mangin double pass and annular blocked sets. Exact rational
singularity checks supplement compensated sums, without a threshold.

LV supplies its own recorded folded geometry and whatever its first-order kernel supplies. Missing OPL is
explicitly unsupported at R3. LV records no hit for a passive visit but keeps a height for every visit under
`recordHeights`; each recorded hit is placed on the one declared step whose kept height is its own, bit for bit,
and the other steps are answered as v2 `unrecordedHits`, which R2 skips (no point is computed). Under the owner's
2026-10-23 decision, run preparation traces exactly the run's generated coarse/fine sets with LV before planning
any engine requests. A recorded interaction past the declared visits refuses the whole run with
`folded-post-path-reflection`, including ref and optiland. Accepted cases record
`folded-declared-path-confirmed:run:<utf8hex>:coarse:<sets>:fine:<sets>` in provenance: the reversible run name
and ordered ray-set hashes, `empty` if requested generation gave no sets, or `unrequested` for an unrequested
resolution. Confirmation covers only these finite sets. No static geometric check or additional sampling is used.
The request-time `lv-folded-post-path-interaction` remains a backstop for direct or unconfirmed requests.
optiland receives native geometry and
reflection models, with read-back conditional policies for active bands and media. Every new read-back has a
purposeful fault test. The remaining LV cardinal defect is returned unchanged and measured by R1.

Sequential compatibility is checked against manifests made from an unchanged `git archive HEAD` extract,
using the same live LV. Case IDs and request IDs are compared job for job. Store keys also cover engine source
fingerprints and adapter revisions: those must change with edited code. Recomputing a HEAD key with the new
engine identity separates that necessary retirement from a changed request. Existing baselines and reports
remain untouched for the owner's Stage 5.3 refresh.

Measured at LV `2e046d71` (clean), optiland `4e893f53` (pre-existing dirty flag), with `--jobs 10`:
16 source runs, **eight confirmed cases and eight source refusals** (seven raw-export refusals and the Maksutov
run's traced-path refusal); **144 jobs, 135 ok, nine unsupported, zero errors**.
Comparison counts include both reference-vs-each and pairwise modes: **149 PASS, 4 FAIL, 87 UNSUPPORTED**;
zero FLOOR, RECORDED, ATTENTION, BLOCKED or ERROR. By rung: R0 40 PASS; R1 13 PASS / 4 FAIL / 23 UNSUPPORTED;
R2 80 PASS; R3 16 PASS / 64 UNSUPPORTED. All four FAIL pairs are
the same LV spherical-primary R1 front-principal error, repeated by the two modes. No R2 or R3 pair fails. LV's
R2 against ref and optiland on its eight measured lenses is within 9.5e-14 mm (hits), 1.9e-13 mm (landings) and
4.0e-15 (directions), with no mask mismatch; LV left 1,474 visits across the 16 requests unrecorded. A serial run
(`--jobs 1`) and `--jobs 10`, each from an empty store, give byte-identical manifests, comparisons and store
entries. Ref–optiland passes every available
R0–R3 pair; first order is unavailable when the declared stop is absent from the path. PASS does not claim an
OPD measurement where no chief ray passes: the off-axis probes have no invented chief, and an obscured axial
chief can be blocked. The ordinary absolute-path gates still apply to the common transmitted rays.

In the following table, P means PASS for every pair of the named engines. F is
`lv-folded-first-order-unavailable` (LV's cardinal function returns null); O is
`lv-folded-optical-path-unavailable`; S is `folded-stop-not-visited`. Each letter means UNSUPPORTED,
not a skipped passing test. A source refusal applies to all four rungs and all three engines.

The diffractive plate is refused with `diffractive-surface`, `tilted-surface`, `surface-profile-unsupported`,
`tilted-image-plane` and `off-axis-image-plane`. Newtonian is refused with `folded-auto-path`,
`surface-profile-unsupported`, `tilted-image-plane` and `off-axis-image-plane`. Diffraction stays Stage 5.2b.
Automatic paths cannot be replaced by one sampled visit list without changing the system.

The historical R1 FAIL was an **LV implementation defect, fixed in LV `f9fb75c86`**, not an unsettled ref result: a single spherical mirror's principal
planes coincide with its vertex. An independent 80-digit Decimal ray at height 1e-30 mm on the actual exported
spherical-primary case gives a focus within 1.25e-63 mm of zero and EFL -100 mm, hence principal z +100 mm.
Before the fix LV returned front-principal z -100 mm, one signed radius away. The source pin identifies its determinant-parity
mistake; ref and optiland independently return +100 mm. The temporary proof is
`/tmp/lvrtc-52-sphere-decimal80.txt`; the fifteen repository ref analytic proofs and native LV pin remain reproducible.
LV stays read-only; the owner fixed it separately in `f9fb75c86`. The [owner brief](upstream/lensvisualizer-mirror-principal-plane.md)
is marked "for the owner; not filed". No gate or floor was widened, and the historical FAIL was not suppressed. The source lines: the folded
matrix is built in physical slope with an unsigned index (`src/optics/first-order/cardinals.ts:213-218`, mirror
step `u' = -u - 2y/R` at `src/optics/math/paraxial.ts:91`), determinant -1 after one reflection, and
`frontPrincipalZ = frontVertexZ + n (D - 1) / C` (`cardinals.ts:126`) assumes determinant +1; for determinant d
the term is `(D - d) / C`. In the exported case the review's own closed form agrees: f = R/2 = -100, F = F' = 0,
H = H' = 100 (the vertex), EFL = F' - H' = -100; LV's A, B, C, D are 1, 100, 0.01, 0, so `(D - 1)/C` = -100.
Ref (signed index, determinant +1) and optiland (`P1()`) both give +100, and the contract's convention (global z,
EFL = F' - H') is applied to every engine's values alike; `test/engines/ref/folded.test.ts` now holds every
first-order value of a single mirror, of a Cassegrain whose stop is its primary, of an inactive-side pass, of a
reverse-medium Mangin and of an entrance pupil at infinity to closed forms.

**Translation gap contained by the owner's traced-ray decision.** LV's explicit traversal is its declared visits
followed by a search of its reflective surfaces with the image plane competing (`gotchas.md`). Contract 1.4 states
fixed visits only. For the raw Maksutov case, 272 of 513 coarse probe rays meet M1 again and stop at SEC's back:
140 of 257 axial rays and 132 of 256 at 0.25 degrees. The whole run is now refused, so ref and optiland have no
jobs or comparisons on that fixed-path substitute. The other eight runs have no recorded post-path interaction
on their coarse sets, whose exact hashes are in each case's provenance. R0–R3 request no fine sets, recorded as
`fine:unrequested`; a rung requesting fine sets has those checked too, before any jobs. Hermetic tests cover a
post-path mirror hit, a hit only on fine sets, exact coarse/fine notes, shared-case notes and serial/pool bytes.
This is finite ray-set confirmation, not a geometric guarantee about other rays. Raw export and census remain
declared-path geometry and carry no run confirmation.

## Stage 5.2b diffractive surfaces

Contract 1.5, rays.trace v3 and first-order v2 implement the owner's 2026-10-25 decision. Only a diffractive case
selects the new definitions. Geometric OPL retains its earlier meaning; signed phase delay is a separate
per-visit array. R0 uses its existing zero shape-mismatch gate for literal phase/order/wavelength metadata. R3
uses unchanged geometric gates plus policy 15's 2e-5-wave phasePath.maxAbs gate. Its basis is the signed local
phase delay on the identical ray, with the existing absolute path accuracy as its scale. No gate is widened and
no floor is added. Policy-14 baselines remain verifiable only across this exact additive change; their bytes and
reports remain untouched. An inapplicable phase figure leaves ordinary comparisons unchanged.

`test/engines/ref/diffractive.test.ts` derives quadratic-phase power/focal length, its height-dependent exact
focus, the local grating equation, signed optical-path contribution, cancellation and a two-line thin hybrid
achromat from closed forms. The exact rational folded-pupil proof includes the requested wavelength's phase
power, so a substrate-only singularity cannot override a finite phase-bearing pupil. Native optiland behavior
is pinned by `workers/python/tests/optiland/test_phase.py`,
including a curved substrate, order/wavelength scaling and each deliberate build fault. LV source canaries hold
geometric OPL capture, its default paraxial wavelength and compiled-coefficient conventions. The binding imports
that wavelength through its manifest and local structural API; the fake tree has a dedicated diffractive plate.
The real LV tier compares exact benchmark/features case identities and R0–R3 request JSON with a read-only HEAD
archive, without tracing another suite or touching any result store.

Reproduce the targeted run with temporary storage only:

```
LVRTC_RUNS_DIR=<temporary-directory> node bin/lvrtc.mjs run suites/diffractive.json --jobs 4
LVRTC_RUNS_DIR=<temporary-directory> node bin/lvrtc.mjs compare diffractive
```

This stage does not certify diffractive MTF. LV's MTF gate still rejects a phase surface. The folded diffractive
plate keeps its tilt/off-axis/profile refusals; no coordinate transform or replacement phase surface is fitted.

## Stage 5.3 catalog suite, classified failures and statistics

`suites/catalog.json` expands every source catalog key through the LV case source, then both zoom ends, without
removing coded refusals: one wide-open / reference-line / design-plane state per prime, two per zoom, and no
photopic, best-focus, stopped-down or middle-zoom state. Engines are lv/ref/optiland; rays use R0–R4 and MTF uses
R4/R4f/R6a. Folded and diffractive cases select their quantity versions automatically. At LV `879c06102` the
selector gives **1,341 states**.

**Current state (owner refresh at frozen LV `879c06102`, optiland `4e893f53`, policy 15).** Every baseline states
policy 15 and its hash; `lvrtc verify` requires exact policy identity of every baseline but the catalog's (the
policy-14 transition and `policy/rungs.v14.json` are gone) and holds 12 baselines with zero problems. The catalog's
two baselines are a dated snapshot, held to the frozen policy they record ([below](#the-catalog-is-a-dated-snapshot)).

| Baseline | Runs | Records | Verdicts |
|---|---|---|---|
| the eight of benchmark, features, states, focus-stations | as before | as before | checked first: no DRIFT, then rewritten |
| `folded.json` (first baseline) | 16 (8 refused at the source) | 96 | 65 PASS / 31 UNSUPPORTED |
| `diffractive.json` (first baseline) | 9 (1 refused at the source) | 96 | 80 PASS / 16 UNSUPPORTED |
| `catalog.json` | 1,341 | 19,761 | 19,690 PASS / 47 UNSUPPORTED / **24 FAIL**, each classified |
| `catalog.mtf.json` | 1,341 (1,297 with an MTF recipe) | 9,079 | 9,079 PASS |

In comparison pairs, both modes, the catalog rays sweep is **71,005: 70,843 PASS / 79 UNSUPPORTED / 83 FAIL /
zero ERROR**, and the catalog MTF sweep **40,944 PASS**. Every FAIL is R2 `mask.mismatches` on one of 12 lenses
(below). No gate or floor moved; the sweep needed no floor, so policy 15 still grants none.
`reports/catalog.{md,json}` are rendered from the two catalog baselines alone.

Catalog baseline annotations use contract minor 6, selected only for these baseline files. `features` comes from
validated cases, `censusReasons` from run/field/recipe codes, and pair `failures` from the exact known-failure list.
The report reads no source census or engine. It distinguishes aggregate pairs and state statuses, deduplicates R4,
counts unsupported codes, interpolates quantiles at `(n - 1)p` over finite gated pair figures (nonfinite ones are
counted apart and rank first) and shows worst-ten runs/engines. FAIL never becomes PASS, and comparison exits 1
even for listed failures. Changed evidence is DRIFT; ERROR is never admitted. Synthetic goldens and CLI tests
cover listing, rejection, round trips, changed figures and report bytes.

`lvrtc statement` remains exactly the eight benchmark/features/states/focus-stations baselines. Folded,
diffractive and catalog baselines do not enter its inventory; the statement's suite scope is a later owner decision.

**Before the sweep: the bounded sample.** A 25-lens / 28-state sample (eleven benchmark lenses, eight feature
lenses, spherical primary, annular mirror, Minolta reflex, a Mirotar source refusal and both Canon DO lenses; three
zooms at both ends) gave **1,248 PASS / 27 UNSUPPORTED** rays pairs and **672 PASS** MTF pairs, zero FAIL/ERROR.
At `--jobs 4` and the default 10 GiB budget, worker peaks were 4.25 GiB rays and 5.70 GiB MTF, with no pauses;
run+compare took 7.25 s rays and 18.07 s incremental MTF, and the compressed store grew by 260,873,483 bytes.
Scaled linearly it estimated 20.2 minutes and 11.6 GiB for the full sweep: an estimate, superseded by the
sweep's counts above, whose wall time and store size this section does not record.

**Folded remeasurement after the owner's fix:** clean LV `879c06102` includes `f9fb75c86`.
`suites/folded.json --rungs r0,r1,r2,r3 --jobs 4`, temporary storage, retains eight accepted / eight refused runs,
144 jobs (135 ok / nine unsupported / zero error). Both-mode counts are R0 40 PASS; R1 17 PASS / 23 UNSUPPORTED;
R2 80 PASS; R3 16 PASS / 64 UNSUPPORTED: **153 PASS / 87 UNSUPPORTED / zero FAIL or ERROR**. The four historical
R1 failures now pass without a gate change. The [principal-plane brief](upstream/lensvisualizer-mirror-principal-plane.md)
